#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I am presenting findings from a 2-year investigation.
2. *(turn 0)* I am presenting evidence that the INR 17.8 trillion (U.S. $218 billion) Indian conglomerate Adani Group engaged in stock manipulation and accounting fraud over decades.
3. *(turn 0)* Gautam Adani is the Founder and Chairman of the Adani Group.
4. *(turn 0)* Gautam Adani has a net worth of roughly $120 billion.
5. *(turn 0)* Gautam Adani added over $100 billion in the past 3 years largely through stock price appreciation in the group’s 7 key listed companies.
6. *(turn 0)* The 7 key listed companies spiked an average of 819% in that period.
7. *(turn 0)* My research involved speaking with dozens of individuals, including former senior executives of the Adani Group.
8. *(turn 0)* My research involved reviewing thousands of documents.
9. *(turn 0)* My research involved conducting diligence site visits in almost half a dozen countries.
10. *(turn 0)* Even if the financials of Adani Group are taken at face value, the 7 key listed companies have 85% downside on a fundamental basis due to sky-high valuations.
11. *(turn 0)* Key listed Adani companies have taken on substantial debt.
12. *(turn 0)* Key listed Adani companies have pledged shares of inflated stock for loans.
13. *(turn 0)* 5 of 7 key listed companies have reported current ratios below 1.
14. *(turn 0)* The group’s very top ranks include 8 of 22 key leaders who are Adani family members.
15. *(turn 0)* The Adani Group has previously been the focus of 4 major government fraud investigations.
16. *(turn 0)* The 4 major government fraud investigations alleged money laundering, theft of taxpayer funds, and corruption totaling an estimated U.S. $17 billion.
17. *(turn 0)* Adani family members allegedly cooperated to create offshore shell entities in Mauritius, the UAE, and Caribbean Islands.
18. *(turn 0)* Adani family members allegedly generated forged import/export documentation to generate fake or illegitimate turnover and siphon money from listed companies.
19. *(turn 0)* Rajesh Adani is Gautam Adani’s younger brother.
20. *(turn 0)* Rajesh Adani was accused by the Directorate of Revenue Intelligence (DRI) of playing a central role in a diamond trading import/export scheme around 2004-2005.
21. *(turn 0)* Rajesh Adani was arrested at least twice over separate allegations of forgery and tax fraud.
22. *(turn 0)* Rajesh Adani was subsequently promoted to serve as Managing Director of Adani Group.
23. *(turn 0)* Samir Vora is Gautam Adani’s brother-in-law.
24. *(turn 0)* Samir Vora was accused by the DRI of being a ringleader of the same diamond trading scam.
25. *(turn 0)* Samir Vora was accused by the DRI of repeatedly making false statements to regulators.
26. *(turn 0)* Samir Vora was subsequently promoted to Executive Director of the critical Adani Australia division.
27. *(turn 0)* Vinod Adani is Gautam Adani’s elder brother.
28. *(turn 0)* Vinod Adani has been described by media as an elusive figure.
29. *(turn 0)* Vinod Adani has been found at the center of government investigations into Adani for alleged role in managing a network of offshore entities used to facilitate fraud.
30. *(turn 0)* My research included downloading and cataloguing the entire Mauritius corporate registry.
31. *(turn 0)* I have identified 38 Mauritius shell entities controlled by Vinod Adani or close associates.
32. *(turn 0)* I have identified entities surreptitiously controlled by Vinod Adani in Cyprus, the UAE, Singapore, and several Caribbean Islands.
33. *(turn 0)* Many Vinod Adani-associated entities have no reported employees, no independent addresses or phone numbers, and no meaningful online presence.
34. *(turn 0)* Many Vinod Adani-associated entities have moved billions of dollars into Indian Adani publicly listed and private entities without required disclosure of related party nature of deals.
35. *(turn 0)* I identified 13 websites created for Vinod Adani-associated entities.
36. *(turn 0)* Many of the 13 websites were formed on the same days and featured only stock photos, naming no actual employees and listing the same nonsensical services such as “consumption abroad” and “commercial presence”.
37. *(turn 0)* I believe the Vinod-Adani shells serve functions including stock parking/stock manipulation and laundering money through Adani’s private companies onto listed companies’ balance sheets.
38. *(turn 0)* Publicly listed companies in India require promoter holdings to be disclosed.
39. *(turn 0)* Publicly listed companies in India require at least 25% of the float held by non-promoters.
40. *(turn 0)* 4 of Adani’s listed companies are on the brink of the delisting threshold due to high promoter ownership.
41. *(turn 0)* My research indicates offshore shells and funds tied to the Adani Group comprise many of the largest “public” (non-promoter) holders of Adani stock.
42. *(turn 0)* Right to Information (RTI) requests I filed with SEBI confirm that offshore funds are subjects of an ongoing investigation.
43. *(turn 0)* The ongoing investigation is more than a year-and-a-half after concerns were initially raised by media and members of parliament.
44. *(turn 0)* A former trader for Elara told me it is obvious that Adani controls the shares.
45. *(turn 0)* The former trader for Elara said the funds are intentionally structured to conceal ultimate beneficial ownership.
46. *(turn 0)* Leaked emails show that the CEO of Elara worked on deals with Dharmesh Doshi.
47. *(turn 0)* Dharmesh Doshi is described as a fugitive accountant who worked closely on stock manipulation deals with Ketan Parekh.
48. *(turn 0)* The leaked emails indicate the CEO of Elara worked with Doshi on stock deals after Doshi evaded arrest.
49. *(turn 0)* Another firm called Monterosa Investment Holdings controls 5 supposedly independent funds.
50. *(turn 0)* Monterosa Investment Holdings’ funds collectively hold over INR 360 billion (U.S. $4.5 billion) in shares of listed Adani companies.
51. *(turn 0)* Monterosa’s Chairman and CEO served as director in 3 companies alongside a fugitive diamond merchant who allegedly stole U.S. $1 billion before fleeing India.
52. *(turn 0)* Vinod Adani’s daughter married the fugitive diamond merchant’s son.
53. *(turn 0)* A once-related party entity of Adani invested heavily in one of the Monterosa funds that allocated to Adani Enterprises and Adani Power.
54. *(turn 0)* A Cyprus-based entity called New Leaina Investments until June-September 2021 owned over U.S. $420 million in Adani Green Energy shares.
55. *(turn 0)* New Leaina Investments’ portfolio comprised about 95% Adani Green Energy shares.
56. *(turn 0)* Parliamentary records show New Leaina was (and may still be) a shareholder of other Adani listed entities.
57. *(turn 0)* New Leaina is operated by incorporation services firm Amicorp.
58. *(turn 0)* Amiicorp formed at least 7 Adani promoter entities.
59. *(turn 0)* Amiicorp formed at least 17 offshore shells and entities associated with Vinod Adani.
60. *(turn 0)* Amiicorp formed at least 3 Mauritius-based offshore shareholders of Adani stock.
61. *(turn 0)* Amiicorp played a key role in the 1MDB international fraud scandal.
62. *(turn 0)* The 1MDB international fraud scandal resulted in U.S. $4.5 billion being siphoned from Malaysian taxpayers.
63. *(turn 0)* A book titled Billion Dollar Whale reported that Amicorp established “investment funds” for key perpetrators that were “simply a way to wash a client’s money through what looked like a mutual fund”.
64. *(turn 0)* My analysis found offshore suspected stock parking entities accounted for up to 30%-47% of yearly “delivery volume” in several Adani listed companies.
65. *(turn 0)* I believe the delivery volume irregularity indicates Adani stocks have likely been subject to “wash trading” or other manipulative trading via suspect offshore entities.
66. *(turn 0)* SEBI has investigated and prosecuted more than 70 entities and individuals over the years, including Adani promoters, for pumping Adani Enterprises’ stock.
67. *(turn 0)* A 2007 SEBI ruling stated that charges against promoters of Adani for aiding and abetting Ketan Parekh entities in manipulating the scrip of Adani stand proved.
68. *(turn 0)* Per the 2007 investigation, 14 Adani private entities transferred shares to entities controlled by Parekh.
69. *(turn 0)* Adani Group responded to SEBI by arguing it dealt with Ketan Parekh to finance the start of its operations at Mundra port.
70. *(turn 0)* I interviewed an individual banned from trading on Indian markets for stock manipulation via Mauritius-based funds.
71. *(turn 0)* The interviewed individual said they knew Ketan Parekh personally.
72. *(turn 0)* The interviewed individual said “all the previous clients are still loyal to Ketan and are still working with Ketan”.
73. *(turn 0)* I found examples of offshore shells sending money through onshore private Adani companies onto listed public Adani companies.
74. *(turn 0)* I found that the funds were used to engineer Adani’s accounting, cushion capital balances, or move capital to other parts of the Adani empire.
75. *(turn 0)* I identified numerous undisclosed related party transactions by both listed and private companies.
76. *(turn 0)* In one instance, a Vinod Adani-controlled Mauritius entity lent INR 11.71 billion to a private Adani entity that did not disclose it as a related party loan.
77. *(turn 0)* The Vinod Adani-controlled Mauritius entity’s INR 11.71 billion loan is described as about U.S. $253 million at that time.
78. *(turn 0)* The private entity subsequently lent INR 9.84 billion to Adani Enterprises.
79. *(turn 0)* The INR 9.84 billion loan is described as about U.S. $138 million at more recent substantially lower exchange rates.
80. *(turn 0)* An offshore shell network includes a UAE entity called Emerging Market Investment DMCC.
81. *(turn 0)* Emerging Market Investment DMCC lists no employees on LinkedIn and has no substantive online presence.
82. *(turn 0)* Emerging Market Investment DMCC is based out of an apartment in the UAE.
83. *(turn 0)* Emerging Market Investment DMCC lent U.S. $1 billion to an Adani Power subsidiary.
84. *(turn 0)* I detail transactions where assets were transferred from a subsidiary of listed Adani Enterprises to a private Singaporean entity controlled by Vinod Adani without disclosure of related party nature.
85. *(turn 0)* Once on the books of the private entity, the assets were almost immediately impaired.
86. *(turn 0)* I believe the impairment likely helped the public entity avoid a material write-down and negative impact to net income.
87. *(turn 0)* Listed Adani companies have seen sustained turnover in the Chief Financial Officer role.
88. *(turn 0)* Adani Enterprises has had 5 chief financial officers over the course of 8 years.

92. *(turn 0)* Records show Shah Dhandharia pays INR 32,000 in monthly office rent.

95. *(turn 0)* Adani Enterprises has 156 subsidiaries.
96. *(turn 0)* Adani’s 7 key listed entities collectively have 578 subsidiaries.
97. *(turn 0)* Adani’s 7 key listed entities engaged in a total of 6,025 separate related-party transactions in fiscal year 2022 alone per BSE disclosures.
98. *(turn 0)* The audit partners at Shah Dhandharia who signed off on Adani Enterprises and Adani Total Gas’ annual audits were as young as 24 and 23 years old when they began approving the audits.
99. *(turn 0)* Gautam Adani claimed in an interview that he has a very open mind towards criticism and that every criticism gives him an opportunity to improve himself.
100. *(turn 0)* The Adani Group has repeatedly sought to have critical journalists or commentators jailed or silenced through litigation.
101. *(turn 0)* The Adani Group has used immense power to pressure the government and regulators to pursue those who question him.
102. *(turn 0)* I believe the Adani Group has been able to operate a large, flagrant fraud in broad daylight in large part because investors, journalists, citizens, and even politicians have been afraid to speak out for fear of reprisal.

#### Facts

1. *(turn 0)* I want to generate a knowledge graph from the provided FAQ and I asked for suggested entity classes to use.
2. *(turn 0)* KrisFlyer (KF) members can use KF miles as payment or partial payment of commercial tickets and selected ancillaries on singaporeair.com (SAA).
3. *(turn 0)* KF members can use this payment option for all commercial booking classes available on SAA.
4. *(turn 0)* KF members can control the number of miles they want to use as long as it is more than the minimum required.
5. *(turn 0)* A minimum of approximately 1050 KF miles is required per transaction (per PNR).
6. *(turn 0)* The SGD to KF miles conversion rate is SGD 1 per 105 KF miles.
7. *(turn 0)* KF members can pay up to 100% of the commercial ticket using miles, inclusive of airport taxes and surcharges.
8. *(turn 0)* Purchase of miles is not available for this payment method; purchase of miles is only available when redeeming award tickets or flight upgrades.
9. *(turn 0)* KF members can use miles to pay for airport taxes and surcharges on commercial tickets bought on SAA.
10. *(turn 0)* KF members can use their miles to pay for the commercial tickets of their nominees (RGN) only.
11. *(turn 0)* KF members can nominate up to a maximum of 5 RGN nominees.
12. *(turn 0)* Once RGN nominees are added, they cannot be removed or changed for a period of 6 months and a fee is applicable.
13. *(turn 0)* KF members can only use their miles for SIA operated flights.
14. *(turn 0)* If an itinerary on SAA includes a codeshare flight operated by another airline, KF miles cannot be used to pay for the SIA operated flight within that itinerary.
15. *(turn 0)* The KF miles payment option is only available for KF members via SAA and not via travel agents.
16. *(turn 0)* Itineraries originating from outside Singapore are priced in local currencies and local fares are converted to SGD before being converted to KF miles using IATA rounding logic.
17. *(turn 0)* KF members can accrue PPS Value, Elite miles, and KF miles when they use miles as payment for their commercial ticket.
18. *(turn 0)* KrisFlyer and Elite miles are earned in proportion to the fare excluding airport taxes paid using credit/debit card if the fare is mileage accruable.
19. *(turn 0)* PPS Value accrued is derived from the proportion paid using credit/debit card.
20. *(turn 0)* KF miles used to pay for a transaction first offset all airport taxes of all the tickets before being used to offset the fare and fuel surcharges.
21. *(turn 0)* Elite miles and KF miles accrued on tickets that are partially paid for using KF miles can only be accrued to the KF account.
22. *(turn 0)* Miles accrued on tickets partially paid for using KF miles cannot be parked with any other OFFPs.
23. *(turn 0)* KF members cannot opt to accrue miles for other KF members before themselves.
24. *(turn 0)* Miles earned from a commercial ticket paid for with KF miles can only be credited to the member’s KF account.
25. *(turn 0)* Commercial tickets paid for with KF miles are strictly non-endorsable.
26. *(turn 0)* Redemption upgrades are allowed for tickets paid for with KF miles as long as the commercial fare is in an upgradeable booking class.
27. *(turn 0)* For flight or itinerary changes, members must contact their local SIA office or call centres.
28. *(turn 0)* Any additional collection resulting from changes can only be paid for using credit card, debit card, or cash.
29. *(turn 0)* Members can submit booking change requests via SAA which are routed offline for handling.
30. *(turn 0)* For seat and meal requests, members can self-service on SAA.
31. *(turn 0)* Offline assistance for issuing tickets paid for with KF miles by AROs/call centres is for exceptional handling only.
32. *(turn 0)* This payment feature is only available on SAA, so if SAA is down KF miles are not available as a payment option.
33. *(turn 0)* Members can continue the transaction offline if SAA is down, but payment must be made using credit/debit card.
34. *(turn 0)* Members can make seat and meal requests on SAA for tickets paid for with KF miles even if the tickets were reissued offline by AROs/call centres.
35. *(turn 0)* Tickets paid for with KF miles can be submitted for refunds on SAA if the tickets are completely unflown and have not been reissued.
36. *(turn 0)* For refunds, the maximum amount paid using credit/debit card is refunded first, not exceeding the original amount paid using credit/debit card.
37. *(turn 0)* The balance of a refund is refunded in miles.
38. *(turn 0)* KF miles that have expired are not refunded.
39. *(turn 0)* For cancellations, the cash amount is refunded first (not exceeding the original amount paid using credit/debit card) before refunding KF miles.
40. *(turn 0)* Residual value refund logic for reissuance from a higher fare to a lower fare refunds cash first and then refunds any balance in miles.
